MONSTER / NPC / ENVIRONMENT CAPTURE CHECKLIST
Capture checkpoint: 20261024-all-completed; cutoff UTC 2026-10-24T04:27:02.320932+00:00
Historical wiki sources are linked beside each entry. Access and encounter triggers may differ today.
Keep both packet and world loggers running before entry, through combat/dialogue/quest changes and effect expiry.
Record map, mode, creature/NPC selected and the action/quest stage. For conditional casts, record the setup too.
Other/unassigned packet sightings do not prove which NPC cast it or that all effects were captured.
Skills seen in activation, damage-attribution or effect/upkeep packets are omitted from this hunting list; their evidence remains in nonplayer-capture-plan.json.
NPCs already observed by name are omitted too. An encounter does not prove every skill/effect was captured.
Passive/environment effects may have no cast-start packet: capture entry, effect application and removal.
Temporary transformations are separate from normally learned player skills. Do not put raw non-player IDs in builds.
A location listed for an NPC is a place to investigate, not proof it casts this skill in every listed encounter.

769 skill/effect entries; 750 have numeric IDs, 19 need ID resolution.
413 entries have a verified NPC Skills-section link; alternative NPC sources are included.
519 search entries remain; 201 observed skills and 261 encountered NPC sources omitted.
This is a reference-backed hunting checklist, not a claim of exhaustive modern monster/encounter coverage.

Alpha Tyrannus
    Locations / encounter context:
      Guild Wars Beyond
      Tarnished Coast
      Alcazia Tangle (during Norn Catering)
    Chomp [2080] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Chomp
        This skill is used by Ceratadons, Ferothraxes, and Tyranni.
        This skill seems to make the creature disappear entirely: it will not display a death emote, nor will it leave a corpse (exploited or otherwise).
        Though it can be used offensively on player-controlled and masterless minions and spirits, creatures that possess this skill will also attempt to use it on their allies for the health gain, including on Raptors.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Alpha_Tyrannus

    Abaddon's Chosen [1457] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Abaddon%27s_Chosen
        This skill is used by Margonites (excluding those in the Domain of Anguish), typically in combination with Abaddon's Favor.
        It will stop all normal damage including hexes and conditions from causing the affected target to drop in health from health degeneration and direct damage.
        Deep Wound still has its usual effect on Margonites under this enchantment.
        Bug.This skill does not prevent life loss caused by life stealing and self-inflicted health loss.
    Abaddon's Favor [1456] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Abaddon%27s_Favor
        This monster skill is used by Margonites, excluding those in the Domain of Anguish.
        Because of its long casting time and the short duration of Abaddon's Chosen, the Margonites will very rarely use this skill to good effect. Even when they are able to use it, it is so easily interrupted that they rarely manage to activate it.
        Bug.Although all enemies take the 70 damage, one enemy will take much more than this. The targeted Margonite seems to gain the amount of health the severely damaged enemy takes.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Apostate

        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Burning_Immunity
        This skill is a passive monster skill, which means it is always in effect.

    Soulrending Shriek [2502] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Soulrending_Shriek
        Used by Bloodthirst Incubus, Stormcloud Incubus, Cryptwing Incubus, and Bloodbath Incubus.
        Being a skill and not a spell, it can remove enchantments such as Shadow Form, Spell Breaker, and Vow of Silence.
        The daze is applied before the damage is dealt, if applicable. Therefore, this skill will also interrupt anyone enchanted who is casting a spell.

    The Chalice of Corruption [1149] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/The_Chalice_of_Corruption
        Is placed on your character for accepting the quest Drink from the Chalice of Corruption and remains on the character until the end of Masters of Corruption.
        It does not get removed by exiting the zone, or even by entering an outpost.
        Even though the effect is present inside outposts, you will not suffer any health degeneration.
        This effect can be removed by abandoning the quest and when under the effects of Loose Magic.

Brrrl
    Locations / encounter context:
      Prophecies
      Kryta
      North Kryta Province

      Istan
      Fahranur, The First City (only during How the Grentches Stole Wintersday)
    Ice Fort [1006] | unclassified non-obtainable; verify access
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Ice_Fort
        This skill is given to all players in Dwayna Vs Grenth and The Great Snowball Fight of the Gods matches, as well as in repeatable Wintersday quests.
        This skill is only available to players during Wintersday events.
        Using this skill results in a small ice structure surrounding the player. If the player moves the ice structure still remains even though the effect will end.
        If a player has a weapon with a 20% of Enchanting mod on it, the enchantment will remain for 12 seconds, but the Ice Fort animation will disappear after 10 seconds.
        Avatar of Grenth's life stealing can bypass this enchantment, since it is not damage.
        The AI cannot exploit the benefits of this skill properly, since they will move while under this enchantment.
        This skill is used by Grentch and Snowman from summoning stones.
        Anomaly.Any effect which would normally cause a knock down will still cause presents to be dropped even if the knock down is prevented.
        Anomaly.If you shadow step out of your Ice Fort, it still protects you from snowballs and their effects.
        Anomaly.Although the description says you are immune to all conditions, blind (from Blinding Snow) and crippled (from Avalanche) can still be applied.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Brrrl

    Invulnerability [538] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Invulnerability
        This skill is a passive, continuously active effect, making it invisible to players. It is used by various NPCs that are not supposed to be fought.
        The description is misleading: This skill prevents others from targeting the NPC (with skills or attacks), gives them immunity to damage (including indirect damage, e.g. from AoE or reflective skills such as Retribution), and provides +20 health regeneration (that bypasses the normal +10 cap; the foe gains 40 health per second)
        Its targeting prevention also stops allies from being able to use beneficial skills on the immune NPC, e.g. allies could not use Orison of Healing.
        It does not prevent Life Stealing or the indirect damage from combining Illusionary Weaponry with skills such as Cyclone Axe.
        It is still possible to kill foes that carry this skill, although it is extremely difficult.
        There are various methods, most of which involve Spiritleech Aura, Vengeful Was Khanhei and Vengeful Weapon, hard mode (so that the foe has a high attack speed), and lots of energy.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Budger_Blackpowder

Bull Trainer Giant
    Locations / encounter context:
      Vabbi
      Holdings of Chokhin
      Vehjin Mines
      Wilderness of Bahdza (after completion of Extinction)
    Stun Immunity [537] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Stun_Immunity
        Earthbind is the only skill that bypasses this skill's effect.
        Most foes that use Giant Stomp carry this passive skill, which prevents them from stunning each other. Similarly, their oversized allies also carry the skill and are unaffected by stomping.
        For example, all Jotun carry the skill and cannot be knocked down by the stomping of Jotun Skullsmashers.
        In contrast, Stone Summit Herders will knock down their dwarf-sized Stone Summit allies, who do not carry the skill.
        Notably, the Sadistic and Nomad Giants of the Desolation do not carry the skill; they can be knocked down like any other foe. Nevertheless, they do not knock each other down.
        Many large foes, such as Wurms, carry Stun Immunity, giving them the appearance of being too big to be knocked over. Other massive foes, e.g. Avalanches, do not carry the skill and can be knocked down.
        The fact that a foe is hard to knock down does not mean they carry this skill. For example, Dhuum and Shiro cannot be knocked down ordinarily, unless the duration of the KD is extended (e.g. via a Stonefist Insignia).
    Also watch for these ordinary player skills on this NPC (capture their setup/effects while here):
      Toxic Chill [1659]
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Bull_Trainer_Giant

Copernicus
    Locations / encounter context:
      Winds of Change
      Kaineng City
      The Undercity (Winds of Change) (only during When Kappa Attack)
    Turtle Shell [1172] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Turtle_Shell
        Many Turtles and Kappas use this skill.
        The damage reduction is equal to the creature's level, e.g. all direct damage to level 23 Kappas will be reduced by 23.
        The reduction takes place after taking into account reductions due to the turtle's armor rating.
        The skill description does not include any activation or recharge time; it is possible that the skill recharges instantly, as turtles have been observed to re-enter the stance quickly.
        Turtle Shell, like other stances, can be removed by Wild Blow, Wild Strike, or Wild Throw (each of which will deal full damage, since the stance is removed first).
        Assassins may use ranks in Critical Strikes to reduce the effect of this stance.
        Repetitive, low damage attacks are ineffective, e.g. hammers are better suited than swords to counter the stance's effect.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Copernicus

Corsair Ghost
    Locations / encounter context:
      Kourna
      Dajkah Inlet
    Invulnerability [538] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Invulnerability
        This skill is a passive, continuously active effect, making it invisible to players. It is used by various NPCs that are not supposed to be fought.
        The description is misleading: This skill prevents others from targeting the NPC (with skills or attacks), gives them immunity to damage (including indirect damage, e.g. from AoE or reflective skills such as Retribution), and provides +20 health regeneration (that bypasses the normal +10 cap; the foe gains 40 health per second)
        Its targeting prevention also stops allies from being able to use beneficial skills on the immune NPC, e.g. allies could not use Orison of Healing.
        It does not prevent Life Stealing or the indirect damage from combining Illusionary Weaponry with skills such as Cyclone Axe.
        It is still possible to kill foes that carry this skill, although it is extremely difficult.
        There are various methods, most of which involve Spiritleech Aura, Vengeful Was Khanhei and Vengeful Weapon, hard mode (so that the foe has a high attack speed), and lots of energy.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Corsair_Ghost

Druid (NPC)
    Locations / encounter context:
      Maguuma Jungle
      Aurora Glade
      Bloodstone Fen
    Invulnerability [538] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Invulnerability
        This skill is a passive, continuously active effect, making it invisible to players. It is used by various NPCs that are not supposed to be fought.
        The description is misleading: This skill prevents others from targeting the NPC (with skills or attacks), gives them immunity to damage (including indirect damage, e.g. from AoE or reflective skills such as Retribution), and provides +20 health regeneration (that bypasses the normal +10 cap; the foe gains 40 health per second)
        Its targeting prevention also stops allies from being able to use beneficial skills on the immune NPC, e.g. allies could not use Orison of Healing.
        It does not prevent Life Stealing or the indirect damage from combining Illusionary Weaponry with skills such as Cyclone Axe.
        It is still possible to kill foes that carry this skill, although it is extremely difficult.
        There are various methods, most of which involve Spiritleech Aura, Vengeful Was Khanhei and Vengeful Weapon, hard mode (so that the foe has a high attack speed), and lots of energy.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Druid_(NPC)

        This is the only known skill in the game which can flip over Siege Turtles, causing them suffer an armor rating penalty, and negating the effects of Turtle Shell for its duration.
        Bug.This skill appears to have a range much larger than "touch".
        Anomaly.This skill has the undocumented effect of bypassing knock down immunity.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Elite_Juggernaut_Klaus

    Stun Immunity [537] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Stun_Immunity
        Earthbind is the only skill that bypasses this skill's effect.
        Most foes that use Giant Stomp carry this passive skill, which prevents them from stunning each other. Similarly, their oversized allies also carry the skill and are unaffected by stomping.
        For example, all Jotun carry the skill and cannot be knocked down by the stomping of Jotun Skullsmashers.
        In contrast, Stone Summit Herders will knock down their dwarf-sized Stone Summit allies, who do not carry the skill.
        Notably, the Sadistic and Nomad Giants of the Desolation do not carry the skill; they can be knocked down like any other foe. Nevertheless, they do not knock each other down.
        Many large foes, such as Wurms, carry Stun Immunity, giving them the appearance of being too big to be knocked over. Other massive foes, e.g. Avalanches, do not carry the skill and can be knocked down.
        The fact that a foe is hard to knock down does not mean they carry this skill. For example, Dhuum and Shiro cannot be knocked down ordinarily, unless the duration of the KD is extended (e.g. via a Stonefist Insignia).
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Enslaved_Frost_Giant

Francis

    Turtle Shell [1172] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Turtle_Shell
        Many Turtles and Kappas use this skill.
        The damage reduction is equal to the creature's level, e.g. all direct damage to level 23 Kappas will be reduced by 23.
        The reduction takes place after taking into account reductions due to the turtle's armor rating.
        The skill description does not include any activation or recharge time; it is possible that the skill recharges instantly, as turtles have been observed to re-enter the stance quickly.
        Turtle Shell, like other stances, can be removed by Wild Blow, Wild Strike, or Wild Throw (each of which will deal full damage, since the stance is removed first).
        Assassins may use ranks in Critical Strikes to reduce the effect of this stance.
        Repetitive, low damage attacks are ineffective, e.g. hammers are better suited than swords to counter the stance's effect.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Francis

Freezie Wannabe

      Istan
      Plains of Jarin (only during White Mist)
    Ice Fort [1006] | unclassified non-obtainable; verify access
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Ice_Fort
        This skill is given to all players in Dwayna Vs Grenth and The Great Snowball Fight of the Gods matches, as well as in repeatable Wintersday quests.
        This skill is only available to players during Wintersday events.
        Using this skill results in a small ice structure surrounding the player. If the player moves the ice structure still remains even though the effect will end.
        If a player has a weapon with a 20% of Enchanting mod on it, the enchantment will remain for 12 seconds, but the Ice Fort animation will disappear after 10 seconds.
        Avatar of Grenth's life stealing can bypass this enchantment, since it is not damage.
        The AI cannot exploit the benefits of this skill properly, since they will move while under this enchantment.
        This skill is used by Grentch and Snowman from summoning stones.
        Anomaly.Any effect which would normally cause a knock down will still cause presents to be dropped even if the knock down is prevented.
        Anomaly.If you shadow step out of your Ice Fort, it still protects you from snowballs and their effects.
        Anomaly.Although the description says you are immune to all conditions, blind (from Blinding Snow) and crippled (from Avalanche) can still be applied.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Freezie_Wannabe

    Wurm Bile [2089] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Wurm_Bile
        This skill is used by Frost Wurms.
        Assuming the condition inflicted does not cause health degeneration or other forms of damage, this skill will deal a total of 800 damage. Bleeding adds another 72 damage, Poison and Disease add 96 damage. Burning will deal 168 damage. Deep Wound may add up to 100 damage depending on maximum health.
    Wurm Siege (Eye of the North) [2543] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Wurm_Siege_(Eye_of_the_North)
        This skill is used by Frostmaw the Kinslayer and Frost Siege Wurms.
        This skill can reach across compass range.
        It deals 50 cold damage if it hits its target and 60 cold damage to foes within the area of its impact; the AoE damage decreases with distance, to a minimum of 1 damage at the edge of earshot range.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Frost_Siege_Wurm

    Stun Immunity [537] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Stun_Immunity
        Earthbind is the only skill that bypasses this skill's effect.
        Most foes that use Giant Stomp carry this passive skill, which prevents them from stunning each other. Similarly, their oversized allies also carry the skill and are unaffected by stomping.
        For example, all Jotun carry the skill and cannot be knocked down by the stomping of Jotun Skullsmashers.
        In contrast, Stone Summit Herders will knock down their dwarf-sized Stone Summit allies, who do not carry the skill.
        Notably, the Sadistic and Nomad Giants of the Desolation do not carry the skill; they can be knocked down like any other foe. Nevertheless, they do not knock each other down.
        Many large foes, such as Wurms, carry Stun Immunity, giving them the appearance of being too big to be knocked over. Other massive foes, e.g. Avalanches, do not carry the skill and can be knocked down.
        The fact that a foe is hard to knock down does not mean they carry this skill. For example, Dhuum and Shiro cannot be knocked down ordinarily, unless the duration of the KD is extended (e.g. via a Stonefist Insignia).
    Stun on Critical Hit [551] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Stun_on_Critical_Hit
        Stun is an internal name for knock down.
        Common on Giants, Wallows, and Wurms.
        Consider bringing skills that prevent critical hits, but keep in mind that several only prevent extra damage from criticals, not bonus effects like stun.
        This skill is a passive, continuously active effect, making it invisible to players.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Furnace_Guardian

    Claim Resource [516] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Claim_Resource
        Claim Resource is a group of skills used by various NPCs throughout the missions of the game and in PvP for numerous things. The skill's title basically explains what it does - the NPC activating it somehow captures or manipulates an object in the game, such as opening a gate or summoning a ship.
        The activation time for this skill varies from case to case; it is often 5 seconds.
        Energy cost likewise differs; often there is none, but there are some exceptions (such as the version used by mages in Ruins of Surmia, which costs 5 energy).
        This skill uses the same skill animation as Fast Casting and No Attribute Mesmer spells.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Ghostly_Hero_(PvE)

    Turtle Shell [1172] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Turtle_Shell
        Many Turtles and Kappas use this skill.
        The damage reduction is equal to the creature's level, e.g. all direct damage to level 23 Kappas will be reduced by 23.
        The reduction takes place after taking into account reductions due to the turtle's armor rating.
        The skill description does not include any activation or recharge time; it is possible that the skill recharges instantly, as turtles have been observed to re-enter the stance quickly.
        Turtle Shell, like other stances, can be removed by Wild Blow, Wild Strike, or Wild Throw (each of which will deal full damage, since the stance is removed first).
        Assassins may use ranks in Critical Strikes to reduce the effect of this stance.
        Repetitive, low damage attacks are ineffective, e.g. hammers are better suited than swords to counter the stance's effect.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Giovanni

    Ice Fort [1006] | unclassified non-obtainable; verify access
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Ice_Fort
        This skill is given to all players in Dwayna Vs Grenth and The Great Snowball Fight of the Gods matches, as well as in repeatable Wintersday quests.
        This skill is only available to players during Wintersday events.
        Using this skill results in a small ice structure surrounding the player. If the player moves the ice structure still remains even though the effect will end.
        If a player has a weapon with a 20% of Enchanting mod on it, the enchantment will remain for 12 seconds, but the Ice Fort animation will disappear after 10 seconds.
        Avatar of Grenth's life stealing can bypass this enchantment, since it is not damage.
        The AI cannot exploit the benefits of this skill properly, since they will move while under this enchantment.
        This skill is used by Grentch and Snowman from summoning stones.
        Anomaly.Any effect which would normally cause a knock down will still cause presents to be dropped even if the knock down is prevented.
        Anomaly.If you shadow step out of your Ice Fort, it still protects you from snowballs and their effects.
        Anomaly.Although the description says you are immune to all conditions, blind (from Blinding Snow) and crippled (from Avalanche) can still be applied.

    Stun Immunity [537] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Stun_Immunity
        Earthbind is the only skill that bypasses this skill's effect.
        Most foes that use Giant Stomp carry this passive skill, which prevents them from stunning each other. Similarly, their oversized allies also carry the skill and are unaffected by stomping.
        For example, all Jotun carry the skill and cannot be knocked down by the stomping of Jotun Skullsmashers.
        In contrast, Stone Summit Herders will knock down their dwarf-sized Stone Summit allies, who do not carry the skill.
        Notably, the Sadistic and Nomad Giants of the Desolation do not carry the skill; they can be knocked down like any other foe. Nevertheless, they do not knock each other down.
        Many large foes, such as Wurms, carry Stun Immunity, giving them the appearance of being too big to be knocked over. Other massive foes, e.g. Avalanches, do not carry the skill and can be knocked down.
        The fact that a foe is hard to knock down does not mean they carry this skill. For example, Dhuum and Shiro cannot be knocked down ordinarily, unless the duration of the KD is extended (e.g. via a Stonefist Insignia).
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Guardian_Serpent

The skill's title basically explains what it does - the NPC activating it somehow captures or manipulates an object in the game, such as opening a gate or summoning a ship.
        The activation time for this skill varies from case to case; it is often 5 seconds.
        Energy cost likewise differs; often there is none, but there are some exceptions (such as the version used by mages in Ruins of Surmia, which costs 5 energy).
        This skill uses the same skill animation as Fast Casting and No Attribute Mesmer spells.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Guild_Thief

Hidden Treasure
    Locations / encounter context:
      Depths of Tyria
      All dungeons except Slavers' Exile and Fronis Irontoe's Lair.
    Invulnerability [538] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Invulnerability
        This skill is a passive, continuously active effect, making it invisible to players. It is used by various NPCs that are not supposed to be fought.
        The description is misleading: This skill prevents others from targeting the NPC (with skills or attacks), gives them immunity to damage (including indirect damage, e.g. from AoE or reflective skills such as Retribution), and provides +20 health regeneration (that bypasses the normal +10 cap; the foe gains 40 health per second)
        Its targeting prevention also stops allies from being able to use beneficial skills on the immune NPC, e.g. allies could not use Orison of Healing.
        It does not prevent Life Stealing or the indirect damage from combining Illusionary Weaponry with skills such as Cyclone Axe.
        It is still possible to kill foes that carry this skill, although it is extremely difficult.
        There are various methods, most of which involve Spiritleech Aura, Vengeful Was Khanhei and Vengeful Weapon, hard mode (so that the foe has a high attack speed), and lots of energy.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Hidden_Treasure

Hill Giant
    Locations / encounter context:
      Kryta
      Kessex Peak
      Kryta
      Riverside Province
      Sanctum Cay
    Stun Immunity [537] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Stun_Immunity
        Earthbind is the only skill that bypasses this skill's effect.
        Most foes that use Giant Stomp carry this passive skill, which prevents them from stunning each other. Similarly, their oversized allies also carry the skill and are unaffected by stomping.
        For example, all Jotun carry the skill and cannot be knocked down by the stomping of Jotun Skullsmashers.
        In contrast, Stone Summit Herders will knock down their dwarf-sized Stone Summit allies, who do not carry the skill.
        Notably, the Sadistic and Nomad Giants of the Desolation do not carry the skill; they can be knocked down like any other foe. Nevertheless, they do not knock each other down.
        Many large foes, such as Wurms, carry Stun Immunity, giving them the appearance of being too big to be knocked over. Other massive foes, e.g. Avalanches, do not carry the skill and can be knocked down.
        The fact that a foe is hard to knock down does not mean they carry this skill. For example, Dhuum and Shiro cannot be knocked down ordinarily, unless the duration of the KD is extended (e.g. via a Stonefist Insignia).
    Stun on Critical Hit [551] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Stun_on_Critical_Hit
        Stun is an internal name for knock down.
        Common on Giants, Wallows, and Wurms.
        Consider bringing skills that prevent critical hits, but keep in mind that several only prevent extra damage from criticals, not bonus effects like stun.
        This skill is a passive, continuously active effect, making it invisible to players.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Hill_Giant

Horde of Darkness (NPC)
    Locations / encounter context:
      The Desolation
      The Alkali Pan (before completing Horde of Darkness)
    Abaddon's Chosen [1457] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Abaddon%27s_Chosen
        This skill is used by Margonites (excluding those in the Domain of Anguish), typically in combination with Abaddon's Favor.
        It will stop all normal damage including hexes and conditions from causing the affected target to drop in health from health degeneration and direct damage.
        Deep Wound still has its usual effect on Margonites under this enchantment.
        Bug.This skill does not prevent life loss caused by life stealing and self-inflicted health loss.
    Abaddon's Favor [1456] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Abaddon%27s_Favor
        This monster skill is used by Margonites, excluding those in the Domain of Anguish.
        Because of its long casting time and the short duration of Abaddon's Chosen, the Margonites will very rarely use this skill to good effect. Even when they are able to use it, it is so easily interrupted that they rarely manage to activate it.
        Bug.Although all enemies take the 70 damage, one enemy will take much more than this. The targeted Margonite seems to gain the amount of health the severely damaged enemy takes.
    Skin of Stone [1623] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Skin_of_Stone
        This is likely to be a constantly active, invisible effect on Hordes of Darkness and the Solitary Colossus.
        This skill only prevents direct damage; it has no effect on health degeneration, life stealing, or any other form of health loss.
        In theory, the Hordes of Darkness could be lured off of the lethal sulfur. However, it is much more efficient to attack them while riding the wurms.
        The only type of wurm siege in the area is the Junundu Siege available to characters riding Junundu.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Horde_of_Darkness_(NPC)

Hungry Kappa
    Locations / encounter context:
      Shing Jea Island
      Sunqua Vale (only during the following quest)
      Quests involved in:
      Shing Jea Island
      Free the Fur
    Turtle Shell [1172] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Turtle_Shell
        Many Turtles and Kappas use this skill.
        The damage reduction is equal to the creature's level, e.g. all direct damage to level 23 Kappas will be reduced by 23.
        The reduction takes place after taking into account reductions due to the turtle's armor rating.
        The skill description does not include any activation or recharge time; it is possible that the skill recharges instantly, as turtles have been observed to re-enter the stance quickly.
        Turtle Shell, like other stances, can be removed by Wild Blow, Wild Strike, or Wild Throw (each of which will deal full damage, since the stance is removed first).
        Assassins may use ranks in Critical Strikes to reduce the effect of this stance.
        Repetitive, low damage attacks are ineffective, e.g. hammers are better suited than swords to counter the stance's effect.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Hungry_Kappa

    Firebomb [2118] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Firebomb
        This is a monster skill used by Ilsundur, Lord of Fire, the boss-like foe at the end of the Catacombs of Kathandrax.
        This skill launches a fire bomb projectile at the target and if it hits, it gives the target a Fire Bomb bundle. If it misses the target, the fire bomb bundle is left on the ground where it hit. In either case, the fire bomb will cause Firebomb Explosion after a set amount of time.
        Ilsundur may also target allies that cannot hold bundles with his skill, including minions or Asura summonings like from Summon Ruby Djinn.
    Shield of Fire [2119] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Shield_of_Fire
        This skill is used by Ilsundur, Lord of Fire, the end boss for Catacombs of Kathandrax, and Charr Effigies.
        Health degeneration and life stealing skills are effective ways to avoid the effects of this skill.

        In contrast, Stone Summit Herders will knock down their dwarf-sized Stone Summit allies, who do not carry the skill.
        Notably, the Sadistic and Nomad Giants of the Desolation do not carry the skill; they can be knocked down like any other foe. Nevertheless, they do not knock each other down.
        Many large foes, such as Wurms, carry Stun Immunity, giving them the appearance of being too big to be knocked over. Other massive foes, e.g. Avalanches, do not carry the skill and can be knocked down.
        The fact that a foe is hard to knock down does not mean they carry this skill. For example, Dhuum and Shiro cannot be knocked down ordinarily, unless the duration of the KD is extended (e.g. via a Stonefist Insignia).
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Island_Guardian

Jade Brotherhood Demolitionist
    Locations / encounter context:
      Beyond
      Winds of Change
      Shing Jea Island
      Seitung Harbor (explorable area) (during Deadly Cargo)
      Kaineng City
      Kaineng Center (explorable area) (during Raid on Kaineng Center)
    Jade Brotherhood Bomb [2755] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Jade_Brotherhood_Bomb
        This skill is used by Jade Brotherhood Demolitionists.
        Damage is dealt as packets of 250. Adjacent foes will be hit three times, nearby foes will be hit twice, and foes in the area will only be hit once.
        This skill's effect can be reduced with the use of damage cappers, or they can be made to waste it against summoned creatures (e.g. by sending an Ebon Vanguard Assassin in first).
        This skill has the highest base sacrifice cost of any skill. Blood of the Master can surpass this with at least 48 friendly minions.
        Unlike other sacrifice skills used by other bosses, if this skill is successfully activated, your party will not receive a Morale Boost from defeating a boss.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Jade_Brotherhood_Demolitionist

    Juggernaut Toss [1155] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Juggernaut_Toss
        This skill is used by Kurzick Juggernauts and Juggernauts.
        This skill, along with Base Defense, cause the longest knock down duration in the regular game (special events have sometimes topped it).
        This is the only known skill in the game which can flip over Siege Turtles, causing them suffer an armor rating penalty, and negating the effects of Turtle Shell for its duration.
        Bug.This skill appears to have a range much larger than "touch".
        Anomaly.This skill has the undocumented effect of bypassing knock down immunity.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Juggernaut_(summon)

Kanaxai
    Locations / encounter context:
      The Jade Sea
      The Deep
      Kanaxai (Zaishen quest)
    Demonic Agility [1152] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Demonic_Agility
        This skill is used primarily by Oni and Kanaxai.
        Combined with the Oni's tendency to popup whenever a foe gets too close, this skill makes Oni devastating for unprepared parties.
        Because this is a monster skill unusable by players, skills such as Revealed Enchantment and Inspired Enchantment will recharge immediately when used to remove this enchantment, resulting in energy gain for the caster.
        Since Oni tend to spawn in groups and they all use this enchantment, Mirror of Disenchantment can help to lower their damage.
    Nightmare Refuge [1311] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Nightmare_Refuge
        It is possible to keep Kanaxai from gaining the benefits of this enchantment using Revealed or Inspired Enchantment despite the 50% spell failure rate. Both these skills instantly recharge as monster skills cannot be copied and thus can be continually used until the enchantment is stripped.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Kanaxai

Kappa (level 1)
    Locations / encounter context:
      Shing Jea Island
      Kinya Province
      Panjiang Peninsula
      Sunqua Vale
    Turtle Shell [1172] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Turtle_Shell
        Many Turtles and Kappas use this skill.
        The damage reduction is equal to the creature's level, e.g. all direct damage to level 23 Kappas will be reduced by 23.
        The reduction takes place after taking into account reductions due to the turtle's armor rating.
        The skill description does not include any activation or recharge time; it is possible that the skill recharges instantly, as turtles have been observed to re-enter the stance quickly.
        Turtle Shell, like other stances, can be removed by Wild Blow, Wild Strike, or Wild Throw (each of which will deal full damage, since the stance is removed first).
        Assassins may use ranks in Critical Strikes to reduce the effect of this stance.
        Repetitive, low damage attacks are ineffective, e.g. hammers are better suited than swords to counter the stance's effect.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Kappa_(level_1)

    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Kappa_(level_6)

    Demonic Agility [1152] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Demonic_Agility
        This skill is used primarily by Oni and Kanaxai.
        Combined with the Oni's tendency to popup whenever a foe gets too close, this skill makes Oni devastating for unprepared parties.
        Because this is a monster skill unusable by players, skills such as Revealed Enchantment and Inspired Enchantment will recharge immediately when used to remove this enchantment, resulting in energy gain for the caster.
        Since Oni tend to spawn in groups and they all use this enchantment, Mirror of Disenchantment can help to lower their damage.

Mage

      Ascalon
      Ruins of Surmia
    Claim Resource [516] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Claim_Resource
        Claim Resource is a group of skills used by various NPCs throughout the missions of the game and in PvP for numerous things. The skill's title basically explains what it does - the NPC activating it somehow captures or manipulates an object in the game, such as opening a gate or summoning a ship.
        The activation time for this skill varies from case to case; it is often 5 seconds.
        Energy cost likewise differs; often there is none, but there are some exceptions (such as the version used by mages in Ruins of Surmia, which costs 5 energy).
        This skill uses the same skill animation as Fast Casting and No Attribute Mesmer spells.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Mage

Mantid Queen (Eye of the North)
    Locations / encounter context:
      Charr Homelands
      Dalada Uplands
      Grothmar Wardowns
      Charr Homelands
      Against the Charr
    False Death [2491] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/False_Death
        A constantly active, invisible effect on mantid insects in the Charr Homelands.
        Resurrection occurs several seconds after death, leading to situations where the mantid may revive itself as your party is already leaving the vicinity.
        This effect can trigger multiple times on the same mantid.
        Mantids that are killed more than once do not provide additional experience or reputation points, and do not refresh kill-based reputation benefits, such as Hunt Rampage.
        Each time a mantid is killed, it leaves behind an exploitable corpse.
    Parasitic Bite [2490] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Parasitic_Bite
        This monster skill is used by enemy Mantids in the Eye of the North expansion.

    Abaddon's Chosen [1457] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.

        This skill is used by Margonites (excluding those in the Domain of Anguish), typically in combination with Abaddon's Favor.
        It will stop all normal damage including hexes and conditions from causing the affected target to drop in health from health degeneration and direct damage.

    Abaddon's Favor [1456] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Abaddon%27s_Favor
        This monster skill is used by Margonites, excluding those in the Domain of Anguish.
        Because of its long casting time and the short duration of Abaddon's Chosen, the Margonites will very rarely use this skill to good effect. Even when they are able to use it, it is so easily interrupted that they rarely manage to activate it.

        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Countdown
        This skill is used by N.O.X..
        N.O.X. will begin using the spell N.O.X.ion Buster shortly after using this skill. While this effect is not listed, Countdown prevents N.O.X.ion Buster (but no other skills) from being interrupted until it ends. The countdown (10 seconds long) is displayed in speech bubbles above N.O.X.'s head, and the skill's effect ends when N.O.X. announces that the countdown has been completed (some time before the spell is cast), making him vulnerable to interrupts again.

Nian
    Locations / encounter context:
      Shing Jea Island
      Panjiang Peninsula (during The Big Bang)
    Invulnerability [538] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Invulnerability
        This skill is a passive, continuously active effect, making it invisible to players. It is used by various NPCs that are not supposed to be fought.
        The description is misleading: This skill prevents others from targeting the NPC (with skills or attacks), gives them immunity to damage (including indirect damage, e.g. from AoE or reflective skills such as Retribution), and provides +20 health regeneration (that bypasses the normal +10 cap; the foe gains 40 health per second)
        Its targeting prevention also stops allies from being able to use beneficial skills on the immune NPC, e.g. allies could not use Orison of Healing.
        It does not prevent Life Stealing or the indirect damage from combining Illusionary Weaponry with skills such as Cyclone Axe.
        It is still possible to kill foes that carry this skill, although it is extremely difficult.
        There are various methods, most of which involve Spiritleech Aura, Vengeful Was Khanhei and Vengeful Weapon, hard mode (so that the foe has a high attack speed), and lots of energy.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Nian

    Demonic Agility [1152] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Demonic_Agility
        This skill is used primarily by Oni and Kanaxai.
        Combined with the Oni's tendency to popup whenever a foe gets too close, this skill makes Oni devastating for unprepared parties.
        Because this is a monster skill unusable by players, skills such as Revealed Enchantment and Inspired Enchantment will recharge immediately when used to remove this enchantment, resulting in energy gain for the caster.
        Since Oni tend to spawn in groups and they all use this enchantment, Mirror of Disenchantment can help to lower their damage.
    Also watch for these ordinary player skills on this NPC (capture their setup/effects while here):
      Shadow Shroud [928]
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Oni

    Reactor Blast Timer [2903] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Reactor_Blast_Timer
        This skill is used by P.O.X..
        Similar to the skill Countdown used by N.O.X., this skill will prevent only the spell Reactor Blast (the equivalent of N.O.X.ion Buster used by N.O.X.) from being interrupted for its duration. The end of the skill's effect can be easily determined, as P.O.X. begins the countdown from 5 in speech bubbles above his head when it begins. When the speech bubble timer reaches 0, the skill's effect has ended and the spell can be interrupted through standard means (interrupt skills or hitting with an attack while he is Dazed).

Polack
    Locations / encounter context:

      Kaineng City
      The Undercity (Winds of Change) (only during When Kappa Attack)
    Turtle Shell [1172] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Turtle_Shell
        Many Turtles and Kappas use this skill.
        The damage reduction is equal to the creature's level, e.g. all direct damage to level 23 Kappas will be reduced by 23.
        The reduction takes place after taking into account reductions due to the turtle's armor rating.
        The skill description does not include any activation or recharge time; it is possible that the skill recharges instantly, as turtles have been observed to re-enter the stance quickly.
        Turtle Shell, like other stances, can be removed by Wild Blow, Wild Strike, or Wild Throw (each of which will deal full damage, since the stance is removed first).
        Assassins may use ranks in Critical Strikes to reduce the effect of this stance.
        Repetitive, low damage attacks are ineffective, e.g. hammers are better suited than swords to counter the stance's effect.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Polack

    Smash of the Titans [3370] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Smash_of_the_Titans
        This skill is used by Frost Titans, Rotting Titans, Armageddon Lords and Titan bosses during the hard mode Titan quests.
        Anomaly."Can't Touch This!" does not work against this touch skill.
    Stun Immunity [537] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Stun_Immunity
        Earthbind is the only skill that bypasses this skill's effect.
        Most foes that use Giant Stomp carry this passive skill, which prevents them from stunning each other. Similarly, their oversized allies also carry the skill and are unaffected by stomping.
        For example, all Jotun carry the skill and cannot be knocked down by the stomping of Jotun Skullsmashers.
        In contrast, Stone Summit Herders will knock down their dwarf-sized Stone Summit allies, who do not carry the skill.
        Notably, the Sadistic and Nomad Giants of the Desolation do not carry the skill; they can be knocked down like any other foe. Nevertheless, they do not knock each other down.
        Many large foes, such as Wurms, carry Stun Immunity, giving them the appearance of being too big to be knocked over. Other massive foes, e.g. Avalanches, do not carry the skill and can be knocked down.
        The fact that a foe is hard to knock down does not mean they carry this skill. For example, Dhuum and Shiro cannot be knocked down ordinarily, unless the duration of the KD is extended (e.g. via a Stonefist Insignia).
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Rotting_Titan

        Most foes that use Giant Stomp carry this passive skill, which prevents them from stunning each other. Similarly, their oversized allies also carry the skill and are unaffected by stomping.
        For example, all Jotun carry the skill and cannot be knocked down by the stomping of Jotun Skullsmashers.
        In contrast, Stone Summit Herders will knock down their dwarf-sized Stone Summit allies, who do not carry the skill.
        Notably, the Sadistic and Nomad Giants of the Desolation do not carry the skill; they can be knocked down like any other foe. Nevertheless, they do not knock each other down.
        Many large foes, such as Wurms, carry Stun Immunity, giving them the appearance of being too big to be knocked over. Other massive foes, e.g. Avalanches, do not carry the skill and can be knocked down.
        The fact that a foe is hard to knock down does not mean they carry this skill. For example, Dhuum and Shiro cannot be knocked down ordinarily, unless the duration of the KD is extended (e.g. via a Stonefist Insignia).
    Stun on Critical Hit [551] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Stun_on_Critical_Hit
        Stun is an internal name for knock down.
        Common on Giants, Wallows, and Wurms.
        Consider bringing skills that prevent critical hits, but keep in mind that several only prevent extra damage from criticals, not bonus effects like stun.
        This skill is a passive, continuously active effect, making it invisible to players.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Sandstorm_Crag

    Demonic Agility [1152] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Demonic_Agility
        This skill is used primarily by Oni and Kanaxai.
        Combined with the Oni's tendency to popup whenever a foe gets too close, this skill makes Oni devastating for unprepared parties.
        Because this is a monster skill unusable by players, skills such as Revealed Enchantment and Inspired Enchantment will recharge immediately when used to remove this enchantment, resulting in energy gain for the caster.
        Since Oni tend to spawn in groups and they all use this enchantment, Mirror of Disenchantment can help to lower their damage.

Sheldon the Verminator
    Locations / encounter context:
      Shing Jea Island
      Panjiang Peninsula (only during Just My Luck)
    Turtle Shell [1172] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Turtle_Shell
        Many Turtles and Kappas use this skill.
        The damage reduction is equal to the creature's level, e.g. all direct damage to level 23 Kappas will be reduced by 23.
        The reduction takes place after taking into account reductions due to the turtle's armor rating.
        The skill description does not include any activation or recharge time; it is possible that the skill recharges instantly, as turtles have been observed to re-enter the stance quickly.
        Turtle Shell, like other stances, can be removed by Wild Blow, Wild Strike, or Wild Throw (each of which will deal full damage, since the stance is removed first).
        Assassins may use ranks in Critical Strikes to reduce the effect of this stance.
        Repetitive, low damage attacks are ineffective, e.g. hammers are better suited than swords to counter the stance's effect.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Sheldon_the_Verminator

    Ice Fort [1006] | unclassified non-obtainable; verify access
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Ice_Fort
        This skill is given to all players in Dwayna Vs Grenth and The Great Snowball Fight of the Gods matches, as well as in repeatable Wintersday quests.
        This skill is only available to players during Wintersday events.
        Using this skill results in a small ice structure surrounding the player. If the player moves the ice structure still remains even though the effect will end.
        If a player has a weapon with a 20% of Enchanting mod on it, the enchantment will remain for 12 seconds, but the Ice Fort animation will disappear after 10 seconds.
        Avatar of Grenth's life stealing can bypass this enchantment, since it is not damage.
        The AI cannot exploit the benefits of this skill properly, since they will move while under this enchantment.
        This skill is used by Grentch and Snowman from summoning stones.
        Anomaly.Any effect which would normally cause a knock down will still cause presents to be dropped even if the knock down is prevented.
        Anomaly.If you shadow step out of your Ice Fort, it still protects you from snowballs and their effects.
        Anomaly.Although the description says you are immune to all conditions, blind (from Blinding Snow) and crippled (from Avalanche) can still be applied.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Snowman_(summon)

Stone Crusher
    Locations / encounter context:
      Echovald Forest
      Arborstone (explorable area)
      Echovald Forest
      Arborstone
    Stun Immunity [537] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Stun_Immunity
        Earthbind is the only skill that bypasses this skill's effect.
        Most foes that use Giant Stomp carry this passive skill, which prevents them from stunning each other. Similarly, their oversized allies also carry the skill and are unaffected by stomping.
        For example, all Jotun carry the skill and cannot be knocked down by the stomping of Jotun Skullsmashers.
        In contrast, Stone Summit Herders will knock down their dwarf-sized Stone Summit allies, who do not carry the skill.
        Notably, the Sadistic and Nomad Giants of the Desolation do not carry the skill; they can be knocked down like any other foe. Nevertheless, they do not knock each other down.
        Many large foes, such as Wurms, carry Stun Immunity, giving them the appearance of being too big to be knocked over. Other massive foes, e.g. Avalanches, do not carry the skill and can be knocked down.
        The fact that a foe is hard to knock down does not mean they carry this skill. For example, Dhuum and Shiro cannot be knocked down ordinarily, unless the duration of the KD is extended (e.g. via a Stonefist Insignia).
    Also watch for these ordinary player skills on this NPC (capture their setup/effects while here):
      Forceful Blow [889]
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Stone_Crusher

      Arborstone (explorable area)
      Morostav Trail
      Echovald Forest
      Arborstone
    Stun on Critical Hit [551] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Stun_on_Critical_Hit
        Stun is an internal name for knock down.
        Common on Giants, Wallows, and Wurms.
        Consider bringing skills that prevent critical hits, but keep in mind that several only prevent extra damage from criticals, not bonus effects like stun.
        This skill is a passive, continuously active effect, making it invisible to players.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Stone_Reaper

Twisted Bark
    Locations / encounter context:
      Echovald Forest
      Urgoz's Warren
    Stun Immunity [537] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Stun_Immunity
        Earthbind is the only skill that bypasses this skill's effect.
        Most foes that use Giant Stomp carry this passive skill, which prevents them from stunning each other. Similarly, their oversized allies also carry the skill and are unaffected by stomping.
        For example, all Jotun carry the skill and cannot be knocked down by the stomping of Jotun Skullsmashers.
        In contrast, Stone Summit Herders will knock down their dwarf-sized Stone Summit allies, who do not carry the skill.
        Notably, the Sadistic and Nomad Giants of the Desolation do not carry the skill; they can be knocked down like any other foe. Nevertheless, they do not knock each other down.
        Many large foes, such as Wurms, carry Stun Immunity, giving them the appearance of being too big to be knocked over. Other massive foes, e.g. Avalanches, do not carry the skill and can be knocked down.
        The fact that a foe is hard to knock down does not mean they carry this skill. For example, Dhuum and Shiro cannot be knocked down ordinarily, unless the duration of the KD is extended (e.g. via a Stonefist Insignia).
    Also watch for these ordinary player skills on this NPC (capture their setup/effects while here):
      Shield Guardian [885]
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Twisted_Bark

Tyrannus
    Locations / encounter context:
      Tarnished Coast
      Alcazia Tangle
      Sparkfly Swamp
    Chomp [2080] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Chomp
        This skill is used by Ceratadons, Ferothraxes, and Tyranni.
        This skill seems to make the creature disappear entirely: it will not display a death emote, nor will it leave a corpse (exploited or otherwise).
        Though it can be used offensively on player-controlled and masterless minions and spirits, creatures that possess this skill will also attempt to use it on their allies for the health gain, including on Raptors.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Tyrannus

    Chimera of Intensity [596] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Chimera_of_Intensity
        Vizier Khilbron casts this on the party three times during Abaddon's Mouth:
        When the first player reaches the Mursaat stronghold at the beginning of the mission.
        When a player successfully gets through the Onyx Gate.
        As a player enters the caldera of the volcano where the Ether Seals are kept.
        Has a two-minute duration.
        Energy cost increases (such as from Quickening Zephyr) and decreases (such as from Renewing Memories) are unaffected by Chimera of Intensity's energy cost reduction.
        Anomaly.The energy cost reduction affects all skills, not just spells.
    Claim Resource [516] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Claim_Resource
        Claim Resource is a group of skills used by various NPCs throughout the missions of the game and in PvP for numerous things. The skill's title basically explains what it does - the NPC activating it somehow captures or manipulates an object in the game, such as opening a gate or summoning a ship.
        The activation time for this skill varies from case to case; it is often 5 seconds.
        Energy cost likewise differs; often there is none, but there are some exceptions (such as the version used by mages in Ruins of Surmia, which costs 5 energy).
        This skill uses the same skill animation as Fast Casting and No Attribute Mesmer spells.
    Invulnerability [538] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Invulnerability
        This skill is a passive, continuously active effect, making it invisible to players. It is used by various NPCs that are not supposed to be fought.
        The description is misleading: This skill prevents others from targeting the NPC (with skills or attacks), gives them immunity to damage (including indirect damage, e.g. from AoE or reflective skills such as Retribution), and provides +20 health regeneration (that bypasses the normal +10 cap; the foe gains 40 health per second)
        Its targeting prevention also stops allies from being able to use beneficial skills on the immune NPC, e.g. allies could not use Orison of Healing.
        It does not prevent Life Stealing or the indirect damage from combining Illusionary Weaponry with skills such as Cyclone Axe.
        It is still possible to kill foes that carry this skill, although it is extremely difficult.
        There are various methods, most of which involve Spiritleech Aura, Vengeful Was Khanhei and Vengeful Weapon, hard mode (so that the foe has a high attack speed), and lots of energy.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Vizier_Khilbron

Wavecrest Stonebreak
    Locations / encounter context:
      The Jade Sea
      Rhea's Crater
    Turtle Shell [1172] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Turtle_Shell
        Many Turtles and Kappas use this skill.
        The damage reduction is equal to the creature's level, e.g. all direct damage to level 23 Kappas will be reduced by 23.
        The reduction takes place after taking into account reductions due to the turtle's armor rating.
        The skill description does not include any activation or recharge time; it is possible that the skill recharges instantly, as turtles have been observed to re-enter the stance quickly.
        Turtle Shell, like other stances, can be removed by Wild Blow, Wild Strike, or Wild Throw (each of which will deal full damage, since the stance is removed first).
        Assassins may use ranks in Critical Strikes to reduce the effect of this stance.
        Repetitive, low damage attacks are ineffective, e.g. hammers are better suited than swords to counter the stance's effect.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Wavecrest_Stonebreak

        The reduction takes place after taking into account reductions due to the turtle's armor rating.
        The skill description does not include any activation or recharge time; it is possible that the skill recharges instantly, as turtles have been observed to re-enter the stance quickly.
        Turtle Shell, like other stances, can be removed by Wild Blow, Wild Strike, or Wild Throw (each of which will deal full damage, since the stance is removed first).
        Assassins may use ranks in Critical Strikes to reduce the effect of this stance.
        Repetitive, low damage attacks are ineffective, e.g. hammers are better suited than swords to counter the stance's effect.
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Young_Turtle

    Stun Immunity [537] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Stun_Immunity
        Earthbind is the only skill that bypasses this skill's effect.
        Most foes that use Giant Stomp carry this passive skill, which prevents them from stunning each other. Similarly, their oversized allies also carry the skill and are unaffected by stomping.
        For example, all Jotun carry the skill and cannot be knocked down by the stomping of Jotun Skullsmashers.
        In contrast, Stone Summit Herders will knock down their dwarf-sized Stone Summit allies, who do not carry the skill.
        Notably, the Sadistic and Nomad Giants of the Desolation do not carry the skill; they can be knocked down like any other foe. Nevertheless, they do not knock each other down.
        Many large foes, such as Wurms, carry Stun Immunity, giving them the appearance of being too big to be knocked over. Other massive foes, e.g. Avalanches, do not carry the skill and can be knocked down.
        The fact that a foe is hard to knock down does not mean they carry this skill. For example, Dhuum and Shiro cannot be knocked down ordinarily, unless the duration of the KD is extended (e.g. via a Stonefist Insignia).
    Stun on Critical Hit [551] | monster/NPC
        Sightings: local 0, other/unassigned 0; attribution/effects still need checking.
        https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Stun_on_Critical_Hit
        Stun is an internal name for knock down.
        Common on Giants, Wallows, and Wurms.
        Consider bringing skills that prevent critical hits, but keep in mind that several only prevent extra damage from criticals, not bonus effects like stun.
        This skill is a passive, continuously active effect, making it invisible to players.
    Also watch for these ordinary player skills on this NPC (capture their setup/effects while here):
      Light of Dwayna [304]
    Creature/NPC reference: https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Zhu_Hanuku

      Visual appearance

      When someone is enchanted with damage reduction enchantments 999 will come up as damage.

      Main article: Dwarven brawling#Game mechanics
      Maximum Energy is initially 15, but increases by 5 every time you recover from a knock out.
      Equipment properties, attributes and consumables will not alter maximum Health, maximum and current Energy and Energy regeneration; they will otherwise work as expected.
      In Hard Mode, Maximum Health is 600, and health gain per enemy defeated is 300.
      You'll take 0 damage from any attack that is not an attack skill.

      Main article: Dwarven brawling#Recommended equipment
      This effect's attacks skills will only be usable when either Brass Knuckles or Thunderfist's Brass Knuckles are equipped.
      Your auto attacks will deal 0 damage with 0 dagger mastery.
      Dagger mastery, customization and other weapon properties will affect the damage of all of your attacks.

      Party members suffer this effect while in the Domain of Pain and during the Gate of Pain; it does not affect allies that are not party members.
      The penalty is applied after skill activation; interrupted, failed, and canceled skills do not trigger the effect.
      The damage is applied before the Divine Favor healing bonus is applied, so monk skills that heal the entire party will keep a primary monk healed while healing the entire party.
      Union affects the damage, reducing it to 5.
    https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Dreadful_Pain

This happens once or twice every 3 seconds to foes that are in range of the statues, depending on the number of statues that the foe(s) is/are in range of.

Godly Yuletide [ID unresolved] | environment
    Packet sightings: local 0; other/unassigned 0 (not complete verification)
    Source unresolved; use the linked article/context before planning a trip.
    Historical context/trigger notes:
      This effect is applied to all players during:
      The Great Snowball Fight of the Gods (PvP)
      Fighting in a Winter Wonderland (Since 2011)
      The description fails to mention that your primary attribute is set to 0 and you cannot attack.
      The use of equipment and consumables only has a limited effect during snowball fights:
      Armor rating has no impact; snowball skills inflict armor-ignoring damage.
      Maximum health or energy aren't modified by equipment, consumables or Morale Boosts.
      Only these armor modifications have any effect: Lieutenant's and Stonefist insignias; runes of Clarity, Purity, Recovery and Restoration.
      Only these weapon modifications have any impact:
      Universal HCT and/or HSR mods will affect Yuletide spells.
      An of Enchanting weapon will lengthen the duration of Ice Breaker and Ice Fort.
      The animation of Ice Fort will always end after 10 seconds. The correct duration of the effect can be seen in the effects monitor.
      Vampiric weapon upgrades will drain health, although they will not allow health to be stolen.
      Silencing weapons will extend the length of dazed; crippling ones will increase the duration of crippled.
      Off-hand inscriptions can reduce the duration of blind, crippled, dazed or disease.
      Furious weapons will increase adrenaline gain.
      Consumables can still provide speed boosts and health regeneration (in PvE).
    https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Godly_Yuletide

Journey to the North [2699] | environment
    Packet sightings: local 0; other/unassigned 0 (not complete verification)
    Source unresolved; use the linked article/context before planning a trip.
    Historical context/trigger notes:
      This buff grants characters a base health of 480 and bonuses to their attributes, and replaces their current armor rating with the max armor rating for that profession until they reach level 20.
      This buff sets one attribute to rank 12 and three additional attributes to rank 9. The effect overrides the character's current ranks in all attributes and places 12 ranks in the attribute of the weapon the character is wielding, 9 ranks in that character's primary attribute, 9 ranks based on the attribute of the focus or shield the character had equipped (if applicable), and 9 ranks based on the skills on the character's skill bar until the character has three attributes with 9 ranks in them.
      If the attribute of the weapon the character is wielding isn't available to that character, no attribute will be set to 12 and instead you will get four attributes with 9 points in each.
      Upon reaching level 20, this buff goes away the next time that character enters a new zone.
      Any runes on your armor which boost attributes are nullified. No part of the rune works, that is, you won't get -75 health for a superior rune which isn't actually doing anything.
      Skills which boost your attributes such as Glyph of Elemental Power or Awaken the Blood have no effect on your attributes while under the effects of this buff.
      Heroes are not affected by this buff.
      Weakness does not reduce your attributes when you are buffed by this.
      Summoning Stones use your actual level.
      Despite the description saying you will be treated as a level 20 character, you still gain experience as if you were your actual, lower level.
    https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Journey_to_the_North

Sulfurous Haze [1384] | environment
    Packet sightings: local 0; other/unassigned 0 (not complete verification)
    Source unresolved; use the linked article/context before planning a trip.
    Historical context/trigger notes:
      This environment effect is received by walking in specific areas in the Desolation. These area roughly cover the yellowy sands, but not exactly. Some yellow areas will not apply the effect, while some brown areas around the yellow sands will apply the effect.
      As with many other mechanical deaths, Sulfurous Haze does not inflict Death Penalty or increase a character's death count.
      Affects characters, henchmen, and heroes. Characters in Junundu, pets, and summoned creatures are immune to this effect and can safely travel across it.
      The death effect is not instantaneous, allowing players to run back to the area of safe ground before being killed.
      The effect is applied once every 2 seconds and affected characters will die if they are still standing on the sulfur when the effect is reapplied. This allows players to survive up to a duration of slightly less than four seconds on the sulfur.
      It is possible to run the Desolation outside of a Junundu with proper knowledge of the safe and unsafe areas (see Guide to the Desolation run for details)
    https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Sulfurous_Haze

    Source unresolved; use the linked article/context before planning a trip.
    Historical context/trigger notes:
      This effect is applied when entering an explorable area or outpost with an incomplete Vanguard Quest in the Quest Log. It disappears when the quest is marked as completed, after the character has left the current area.
      Since vanguard quests are lost when leaving pre-Searing Ascalon, the effect is only available there.
      Whilst under this effect:
      The base armor of shields provides no additional armor.
      Additional armor modifiers on weapons and offhands, such as the +7 armor vs. piercing damage on the staff from The Wayward Wizard, do provide additional armor.
      The armor boost is active vs. all foes level 6 and higher.
      Profession specific armor bonuses, such as the Warrior's +20 armor vs. physical damage and the Ranger's +30 armor vs. elemental damage, are automatically applied by the buff. This allows you to remove armor pieces and still maintain profession specific armor passives through the buff.
      Armor-boosting insignias are still effective vs foes level 5 and lower but are overwritten and ineffective vs foes level 6 and higher.
    https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Vanguard_Initiate

Yuletide [1004] | environment
    Packet sightings: local 0; other/unassigned 0 (not complete verification)
    Source unresolved; use the linked article/context before planning a trip.
    Historical context/trigger notes:
      This effect is applied to all players during Dwayna Vs Grenth matches and all repeatable Wintersday quests (excluding Fighting in a Winter Wonderland).
      During Snowball Dominance, your pet will accompany you if you begin with Heal as One, Charm or Comfort Animal equipped. It will not deal damage, but can be targeted by foes. If it dies, your skills will still be disabled.
      The use of equipment and consumables only has a limited effect during snowball fights:
      Armor rating has no impact; snowball skills inflict armor-ignoring damage.
      Maximum health or energy aren't modified by equipment, consumables or Morale Boosts. During Snowball Dominance, Journey to the North will increase the character's health to 680 and primary attribute rank to 9; however, Heart of the Norn has no effect.
      Only these armor modifications have any effect: Lieutenant's and Stonefist insignias; runes of Clarity, Purity, Recovery and Restoration.
      Only these weapon modifications have any impact:
      Universal HCT and/or HSR mods will affect Yuletide spells.
      An of Enchanting weapon will lengthen the duration of Ice Breaker and Ice Fort.
      The animation of Ice Fort will always end after 10 seconds. The correct duration of the effect can be seen in the effects monitor.
      Vampiric weapon upgrades will cause -1 health regeneration; although, they will not allow health to be stolen since you cannot attack.
      Silencing weapons will extend the length of dazed; crippling ones will increase the duration of crippled.
      Off-hand inscriptions can reduce the duration of blind, crippled, dazed or disease.
      Furious weapons will increase adrenaline gain.
      Consumables can still provide speed boosts and health regeneration (in PvE).
    https://wiki.guildwars.com/wiki/Yuletide

Amulet of Protection [896] | monster/NPC
    Packet sightings: local 0; other/unassigned 0 (not complete verification)
    Source unresolved; use the linked article/context before planning a trip.
    Historical context/trigger notes:

      Effect: Limits the amount of damage he can take per second. If the Guild Lord loses health due to damage-over-time effects or other direct loss of health, then once every second, it heals him for the amount of health he lost that exceeds the limit.
